tiyākarāyar nakar, ceṉṉi - 600 017.
to.pē.: 24339030, cel: 9444410654
miṉṉañcal: elavazhagantm@gmil.com


yāṉ aimpatu āṇṭukaḷukku mēlāka ērāḷamāṉa nūlkaḷil ārāyntu kaṇṭa muṭivukaḷi nūlkaḷāka eḻutiyi rukkiṉṟēṉ.
pāṟkaṭalik kaṭintu amutam eṭuttatu pōla, uṇmi kāṇum uṇarcciyiṉāl naṭunili niṉṟu ārāyntiruk kiṉṟēṉ. 
oruvar tam vāḻnāḷ muḻuvatum kaṟṟālum aṟiya muṭiyāta uṇmikaḷi ellām eḷitāka eḻuti vit tirukkiṉṟēṉ.
ellārum tamiḻ ilakkiyaṅkaḷyāvaṟṟiyum kaṟkum tolliyi mēṟkoḷḷa vēṇṭiyatilli, atu muṭiyavum muṭiyātu, tēviyumilli. 
ellā nūlkaḷilum uḷḷa ciṟanta uṇmikaḷi ellām piḻi cāṟāka yāṉ vaṭittut tantuḷḷēṉ.
eṉ nūlkaḷip paṭittāṟpōtum, ataṉāl, tamiḻ muḻutuṅ kaṟṟa payaṉi aṭiyalām.
- maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ



cāṉṟitaḻ
nāki - tiru. vētācalam piḷḷiyavarkaḷ orupōtu tiruvaṉantapurattil oruvāran taṅkiyirunta ñāṉṟu, avaruṭaṉ neruṅkip paḻakum vāyppuk kiṭittataṟkuc cālavum makiḻkiṉṟēm. varuṅkālat tamiḻ makkaḷukku maṭṭaṟṟa tamiḻppulami mikuntu varumeṉpataṟkuc cāṉṟāka ivar iḷamiyilēyē nuṇmāṇ nuḻipulam uṭiyarāyiruppatu kaḷippūṭṭukiṟatu. maṟṟum tāymoḻip paṟṟuk kuṟinta ikkālattil, ivar tamatu nuṇṇaṟivit tamiḻ nūlkaḷil muṟṟum celutti naṉkārāyntuḷḷatum pārāṭṭaṟkuriyatu. pattuppāṭṭu, kalittoki, cīvakacintāmaṇi mutaliya paḻantamiḻ nūlkaḷil ārāycciyuṭaṉ kūṭiya ivartam aṟivuṭimiyē eṉṉi ivarpāl matippuṟumāṟu ceytatu. tamiḻāciriyarkaḷiṉ toki curuṅkivarum ikkālattil ivarukkuc ciṟanta ūkkamaḷittāl ivar oru ciṟanta tamiḻāciriyarāvāreṉṟu nām uṟutiyāka nampukiṉṟēm. muṉṉarē iraṇṭāntarak kallūrikaḷil tamiḻkaṟpikkap pōtumāṉa aḷavu ilakkaṇa ilakkiyattiṟ ciṟanta tērcci peṟṟavarāyirukkiṉṟāreṉak karutukiṉṟōm. Fiwªj msÉš v~¥, V., (F.A.) vakuppukkuriya āṅkila aṟivu peṟṟārāṉāl mēlnāṭṭu muṟippaṭi tamiḻ nūlkaḷi varalāṟṟuc cāṉṟuṭaṉ ārāyavum eḻutavum vallunarāvāreṉpatu emmuṭiya ciṟanta eṇṇam.

tirunelvēli	tamiḻttāy vāḻttiṉ  āciriyar
2-12-1895
(inta naṟcāṉṟu tirunelvēli intuk kallūriyil cuntaram piḷḷiyavarkaḷ pērāciriyarāka iruntapōtu koṭukkap peṟṟatu.)


tamiḻkkāvalar -  maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ
paṇṭit tamiḻ nūlkaḷik kaṇṭavar eṉṟu cāmināta ayyariyum, tamiḻ nāṭakaṅ kaṇṭavareṉṟu cuntaram piḷḷiyiyum, civan taḻikka vanta vāṉmukil eṉṟu ñāṉiyāraṭikaḷiyum, camayaṅkaḷiṉ camaracaṅ kaṇṭavareṉṟu tiru.vi. kaliyāṇacuntarariyum, palkali kaṇṭa aṟiñar eṉak kā.cuppiramaṇiya piḷḷiyiyum, kaviccuvi kaṇṭavareṉac citamparanātariyum, tamiḻk kalikaḷ taḻikka vanta pērāciriyar eṉṟu kalki (uh.kiUZz_®¤â)iaí«, pulattuṟi muṟṟiya nalattaku cāṉṟōreṉa vēṅkaṭacāmi nāṭṭāriyum, tamiḻ oliyārāycciyiṉ nuṭpaṅ kaṇṭāreṉṟu māṇikka nāyakkariyum, ilakkiya nalaṅkaṇṭa ēntaleṉṟu katirēcaṉ ceṭṭiyāriyum, vāravaḻipāṭukaṇṭa vaḷḷalār  eṉṟu caccitāṉantam piḷḷiyiyum, nāṭṭuṇarvūṭṭiya nallicip pāvalareṉap pāratiyāriyum pārāṭṭiya tamiḻulakam, irupatām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ iṇiyillāp pulavarāṉa aṭikaḷit taṉittamiḻ kaṇṭa tamiḻk kāvalar eṉa uṇarcciyuṭaṉ pōṟṟukiṉṟatu. 
 - pērāciriyar maṟi. tirunāvukkaracu
maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ varalāṟu (pak 302)

maṟimaliya māṇpu
nuḻivuri
taṉittamiḻ eṉṟa aḷavil muṉ niṟkum muḻuturu eḻilvaṭivar
tavattiru. maṟimaliyaṭikaḷārē āvar!
munti aṭikaḷ ākiya iḷaṅkōvaṭikaḷ, `muttamiḻ aṭikaḷ eṉa muḻakkamiṭa niṉṟār!
irupatām nūṟṟāṇṭu maṟimaliyaṭikaḷō, taṉittamiḻt tanti eṉṉum takavārnta perumi peṟṟār.
tamiḻ taṉittu iyaṅkātu eṉṟum, vaṭamoḻi vaḻiyatu eṉṟum, vaṭamoḻit tuṇiyiṉṟi iyaṅkātu eṉṟum, ulaka moḻikaḷuk kellām tāymoḻi vaṭamoḻiyē eṉṟum  tamiḻk kalvi valla peṟṟiyarum muṟṟum mayaṅki yurikka,
vaṭamoḻi ulaka moḻikaḷukkut tāymoḻi eṉṟāl, ataṟkum maṟṟi moḻikaḷukkum tanti moḻi tamiḻē eṉṟu kūṟiya vaṭalūr aṭikaḷār pukaḻparappāḷarāyp potunilik kaḻakam kaṇṭa tava aṭikaḷār, taṉittamiḻt tanti eṉṉum perumi peṟṟa maṟimaliyaṭikaḷār āvar.
civaṉiyam cārnta perumakkaḷum, pēraṟivarkaḷum kūṭa, oppārāy, `taṉittamiḻ (taṉittu amiḻ) eṉṟu paḻikkavum iḻikkavum, tuṇintu niṉṟu, avar veḷka; veḷḷiṭi maliyākat `taṉittamiḻ eṉpati nili nāṭṭiya niṟimali, maṟimaliyaṭikaḷārē!
civaṉiya maṭattiṉarum, vaṭamoḻi vallārum, `tol kāppiyak  kaṭal eṉap pukaḻappaṭṭārumām cāmināta tēcikar, nāṇamiṉṟi, 
ainteḻuttāl orupāṭi eṉṟu aṟiyavē nāṇuvar
aṟivuṭiyōrē; yāmum atuvē
eṉṟum,
oṉṟē āyiṉum taṉittamiḻ uṇṭō?
eṉṟum - eḻutip parappiṉārāka,
oru col tāṉum vēṟṟuc col viravā nūl yām ceyvēm eṉṟu aimpāṉ nāṉku nūlkaḷ kalimali pēḻiyāy eḻutik kāṭcip paṭuttiya māṭciyar maṟimaliyaṭikaḷār!
tolkāppiyak kāval
tamiḻ moḻikku vāytta tolkāppiya vaḷanūl,
vaṭacoṟ kiḷavi vaṭaveḻut torīi
eḻuttoṭu puṇarnta collā kummē	(884)
eṉṟum,
moḻi peyarppuc ceytalilum muṟimi pēṇa vēṇṭum eṉpati,
moḻipeyarttu atarppaṭa yāttal	(1597)
eṉṟum,
pēccu vaḻakkil oru kāl vēṟṟuccol vantu viṭiṉum,
marapunili tirital ceyyuṭ killi 	(1590)
eṉṟum - varampāṇi ceytum, ac cevviyal vaḻittaṭam citintum cīraḻintum, cēṟum cakatiyum nāṟum ciṟumiyum kuṟumiyum vaḻivaḻiyē eyta mūvāyiram āṇṭukaḷāy, ayalōr cūḻcciyālum tiṟavōr mayakkālum ēṟpaṭṭa aḻipāṭukaḷi ellām tāmē ōr iyakkamāy iruntu, viḻippum eḻucciyum ūṭṭiyavar maṟimali aṭikaḷē eṉpatāl,
āriyattiṉiṉṟu tamiḻi mīṭṭeṭuppa taṟkāka arum pāṭupaṭṭu ilakkiya ilakkaṇa marapōṭu yāṉ kaṟṟa moḻikaḷ muppatu eṉṉum pāvāṇar,
mūvā yiramāṇṭu mōtum vaṭamoḻiyāl
 cāvām takiniṉṟa taṇṭamiḻi - mēvāk
 kumarit taṉinilikkuk koṇṭuvanta  appar
 amarar maṟimali yār
eṉap pāṭip paraviṉār. `taṉit tamiḻt tantiyār eṉṟa pārāṭṭum ip pāṭṭiṉ talippil vaḻaṅkiṉār.
nuṇṇiya nūlpalavum kaṟpatē nōṉpāka
 eṇṇiya vāḻnāḷ elāmaḷittup - paṇṇiya
 centamiḻnūl ovvoṉṟum ceppum kaṭalaṟivi
 pintamiḻ kākkum paṭi
eṉa aṭikaḷ kalvi mēmpāṭṭiyum kalit tiṟap paṭippukkaḷiṉ mēmpāṭṭiyum pāvēntar pāratitācaṉār pārāṭṭukiṟār.
paltuṟi āṟṟal
aṭikaḷiṉ paltuṟi āṟṟali 1. pērāciriyar, 2. perum pulavar, 3. pāvalar, 4. ārāycciyāḷar, 5. mummoḻip pulavar, 6. moḻipeyarppāḷar, 7. coṟpoḻi vāḷar, 8. eḻuttāḷar, 9. palkalic celvar, 10. taṉittamiḻt tantiyār eṉṟu pāvāṇar pakuttuk kāṭṭukiṟār.
mēlum avar tam nuṇmāṇ nuḻipulatti
paṉimali mukaṭṭiṉ uyarvum, nīla āṟṟiṉ nīḷamum amiti vāriyiṉ āḻamum amintavar maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ eṉkiṟār. (paṉimali  - imayam; nīla āṟu - nil; amitivāri - pacipikku mākaṭal).
aṭikaḷār nūlkaḷ
aṭikaḷ paṉmāṇ māttamiḻ nūlkaḷi, nūlaka iyakkat tanti araṅka nātarum, innāḷ civacittānta nūṟpatippuk kaḻaka āṭciyarum annāḷ nūlakarumākiya tirumiku irā. muttuk kumāracāmi avarkaḷum 14 vakimippaṭutti vittami perum pārāṭṭukku uriyatām avi,
1. maruttuvam  2. maṟiporuḷ  3. ilakkiyam 4. itaḻkaḷ  5. caṅka  ilakkiyam  6. pāṭal 7. nāṭakam  8. putiṉam 9. kaṭitam 10. kaṭṭuri 11. camayam  12. tattuvam 13. varalāṟu 14. camūka iyal eṉpaṉa.
mutaṟ kuṟaḷ vāta nirākaraṇam eṉṉum maṟuppu nūlē, aṭikaḷiṉ mutal nūlāka veḷippaṭukiṉṟatu (1898) annūṉ maṟuppu nayamē nāki aṭikaḷi - pallavapura aṭikaḷāka -taṉittamiḻ aṭikaḷāka ākka arum perum tūṇṭal āyiṟṟām.
aṭikaḷār varalāṟu: kīḻkaṭal cār nākappaṭṭiṉattic cārnta ciṟṟūr kāṭampāṭi, āṅku vāḻnta aruntavap peruntakiyar cokkanātar - ciṉṉammiyār! ivviṇiyar koṭiyāyt tamiḻaṉṉi ceyta tavap pēṟṟāl piṟantavar vētācalam. piṟanta peru maṅkala nāḷ ki.pi. 15.07.1876.
cokkanātar, cittamalam aṟuvitta cittamaruttuvar; aṟiva maruttuvam eṉpatum atu. attoḻil avarkku vaḷatti vaḻaṅkiyatu. ataṉāl vētācalam kuṟivaṟa vāḻntār.
nākappaṭṭiṉattu veciliyaṉ micaṉ paḷḷiyil aintu vakuppuvari payiṉṟār; ciṟanta māṇavarāka viḷaṅkiṉār. āṟām vakuppil cērum niliyil, arumit tantiyār iyaṟki eytiṉār. āyiṉum aṉṉiyār uṟutiyāl kalviyit toṭarntār. appaḷḷiyil āṅkilak kalvikku irunta mutaṉmi tamiḻukku illi. kiṟittava camayam paṟṟi aṉṟic civaṉiyam paṟṟi aṟiya vāyppilli eṉṉum iraṇṭu kuṟikaḷiyum iḷamiyilēyē uṇarntār.
vīṭṭilēyē tamiḻ nūlkaḷip payiṉṟu mutaṟ kuṟiyiyum, nākiyil irunta intumatāpimāṉa caṅkam vaḻi iraṇṭām kuṟiyiyum nīkkiṉār. paḷḷiyilum kuṟiyaṟak kaṟṟār. varuvāyk kuṟiyāl vāṭum aṉṉikkuc cumiyāy illāmal taṉiyē payilvatē iṉiyavaḻi eṉa eṇṇi, nākiyil puttaka vaṇikarāka viḷaṅkiya nārāyaṇa cāmiyār pulamiyālum ciṟantu viḷaṅkiyavar. ātalāl avariṭam taṉiyē ilakkiya  - ilakkaṇa - camaya nūlkaḷik kaṟṟār. nārāyaṇariṉ muṉṉi māṇavar pērā. maṉōṉmaṇīyam pe. cuntaraṉār eṉpatiyum aṟintār. oṉpatām vakuppoṭu paḷḷik kalviyi niṟuttit taṉikkalviyē kaṟṟār. tolkāppiyam, toki nūlkaḷ, tirukkuṟaḷ, tirucciṟṟampalak kōvi, iṟiyaṉār akap poruḷ eṉakkaṟṟār.
aṭikaḷār iyaṟṟiya tiruvoṟṟi murukar mummaṇik kōvi mukappil, ematu patiṉintām āṇṭil muṟippaṭi tuvaṅkiya tamiḻp payiṟciyāṉatu ematu irupattōrām āṇṭiṟkuḷ tolkāppiyam, tirukkuṟaḷ, ciṟṟampalak kōviyār eṉṉum mūṉṟu nūlkaḷiyum muḻutum neṭṭuruc ceytu muṭittēm eṉṟum vēṟucila nūlkaḷiyum muḻuvatāka ōtiyatiyum kūṟi,
iṅṅaṉamāka, viḻumiya tamiḻppaḻa nūlkaḷil ematu karuttu īrppuṇṭu niṉṟu payiṉṟa payiṟciyiṉālēyē ceyyuḷum uriyum taṉic centamiḻ naṭiyil eḻutum tiṟam ematu iḷamip paruvattilēyē emakku vāyppa tāyiṟṟu eṉkiṟār. avvāṇṭu ki.pi. 1897. nāki intu matāpimāṉa caṅkattiṟkuc civa cittānta caṇṭa marutam eṉṉum virutu peṟṟa cōmacuntara nāyakar vantu coṟpoḻivāṟṟiṉār. avar poḻivil vanta karuttoṉṟaṉi maṟuttu oruvar eḻuta, ataṟku maṟuppāka murukavēḷ eṉṉum puṉipeyaril maṟuppuri eḻutiṉār. ataṉikkaṟṟa nāyakar, mīḷap poḻivukku varuṅkāl muruka vēḷic cantikka virumpiṉār. accantippiṉ payaṉē uṉṉi virivilē ceṉṉikku varuvippōm. nī antap pakkaṅkaḷil iruppatē nallatu eṉṟu kūṟiṉār. ituvē aṭikaḷārkkuc ceṉṉi vāḻvu amiya mutal tūṇṭal āyiṟṟu. aṭikaḷukkup patiṉēḻām vayatil (1893) tirumaṇam nikaḻntatu. tuṇivi tam māmaṉ makaḷ cavuntara valli eṉpār.
tiruvaṉantapuram
nārāyaṇacāmi avarkaḷiṉ muṉṉi māṇavarām pērā. cuntaraṉār tiruvaṉanta purattil iruntār. avariyaṟṟiya maṉōṉmaṇīya nāṭaka nūl kaṟṟu ataṉ arumi kaṇṭu, pērāciriyarik kāṇavirumpiṉār. ataṉāl tam āciriyarōṭu tiruvaṉantapuram ceṉṟu pērāciriyarōṭu uriyāṭiṉār. vētācalaṉār iḷam paruvattiyum mūtaṟiviyum aṟinta cuntaraṉār viyappuṟṟut takutic cāṉṟu oṉṟu vaḻaṅkiṉār. ciṟitu kālam tiruvaṉanta purattil āciriyap paṇiyum ceytār. avviṭattiṉ paruvanili ovvāmiyāl nākikkē tirumpiṉār.
ceṉṉi: cōmacuntaraṉār muṉṉē kūṟiyapaṭi vētācalaric ceṉṉikku aḻittu aṟiñar nallucāmi eṉpār naṭāttiya cittānta tīpiki eṉṉum itaḻāciriyar ākkiṉār. piṉṉē ceṉṉi kiṟittavak kallūrikku oru tamiḻp pērāciriyar vēṇṭiyiruttal aṟinta cōmacuntaraṉār vētācalaṉāri akkallūrip pērāciriyarākka muyaṉṟār. atukāl tamiḻttuṟit talivarāka iruntavar vi.kō cūriyanārāyaṇa cāttiriyār eṉṉum paritimāl kaliñar. kallūri mutalvar villiyam millar eṉpār.
9.3.1898 il vētācalaṉār paṇiyēṟṟār. aṅkup patiṉmūṉṟu āṇṭukaḷ paṇiyāṟṟiyuḷḷār.
akkallūrik kālattē iḷaṅkali māṇavarkkup pāṭamāka irunta mullippāṭṭu (1903) paṭṭiṉappāli (1906) eṉpavaṟṟukku ārāycci uri eḻutiṉār.
avvuri nūli vāṅkik kaṟṟa māṇākkarum ēṉit tamiḻaṟiñarum putumuṟiyāka eḻutappaṭṭa avvurip pāṅkiṉip piṉpaṟṟip paḻiya tamiḻ nūlkaḷi ārāyvatil kiḷarcci peṟalāṉār. ipputumuṟi uri, tamiḻ nūlārāyum vakikaḷi iṉitu viḷakki aṟiñar palarkkum aka makiḻcci yiṉi viḷittami kaṇṭu iṉi, ittakiya urikaḷiyē eḻututal vēṇṭum eṉa ematuḷḷam peritum viḻintatu eṉkiṟār.
ceṉṉip palkalik kaḻakattārāṅkilamum vaṭa moḻiyum kaṭṭāya mākap paṭittal vēṇṭum eṉṟum, tāymoḻip pāṭatti viruppap pāṭamākak kaṟpikkalām eṉṟum oru tīrmāṉam āṭcikkuḻuvil koṇṭu vantaṉar. oru pāṭam viruppappāṭam eṉappaṭṭa piṉṉar ataṉik kaṟka varuvār evar? tamiḻ kaṟkum māṇavar eṇṇikki kuṟiyavē āciriyap paṇiyiṭamum kuṟiya vēṇṭuvatāki, aṭikaḷār 31.1. 1911 ām nāḷuṭaṉ kallūrip paṇiyi muṭikka vēṇṭiya tāyiṟṟu.
māṇavar: ikkālattil aṭikaḷiṉ māṇavarāka iruntāruḷ, taṇikimaṇi ceṅkalvarāyar, iracikamaṇi ṭi.kē.ci, nāvalar ca.cō. pārati, pēraṟiñar ca. viyāpuriyār mutalōr cilar.
taṇikimaṇiyiṉ, tēvāra tiruvācakac collaṭivu oru piṟappaḷavil ceyyak kūṭiyatā?
nāvalar ca. cō. pāratiyāriṉ āyvut tuṇivu ettaṉi kacaṭukaḷi nīkkiyatu? viyāpuriyār, kāla āyvilum, vaṭamoḻip piṭivātattilum tammic cālpu āyvil iruntu vilakkiṉālum, caṅka ilakkiyap patippu, nikaṇṭuppatippu, palkalik kaḻaka akaramutalip patippu ivaṟṟic ceyta arumi eṉṉa? puṟattiraṭṭu, kaḷaviyal, kāriki āyavaṟṟiṉ patippuc ciṟappu eṉṉa?
ivarkaḷ paṇiyum aṭikaḷārit taḻuvutal uṇmi tāṉē!
camaraca caṉmārkkam
kallūrip paṇiyil iruntu viṭupeṟṟa aṭikaḷār, pallavapuratti vāḻviṭamākak koṇṭār. camaraca caṉmārkka niliyam tōṟṟuvittār. 
ellāc cātiyārkkum ellāc camayattārkkum aṅkīkāramāṉa cīvakāruṇiya oḻukkattiyum, camaraca caṉmārk kattiyum piṟkālattil mika valiyuṟuttip pōtitta āciriyar srīmat irāmaliṅka cuvāmikaḷē āvār. accuvāmikaḷ valiyuṟuttiya avviraṇṭu koḷkikaḷiyum eṅkum valiyuṟutti viḷakki aṉparkaḷi oruṅku kūṭṭa vēṇṭi accuvāmikaḷ iṭṭa peyarālēyē, `camaraca caṉmārkka niliyam eṉṉum itu tāpikkalāyiṟṟu. inniliyattiṟku muṉ āciriyar tiruvaḷḷuva nāyaṉār; piṉ āciriyar irāmaliṅka cuvāmikaḷ āvar eṉṟu peyarīṭu ceytu koḷkikaḷiyum vakukkiṟār.
kuṭumpam
1898il aṭikaḷār ceṉṉikku varumpōtu avartam aṉṉiyār ciṉṉammiyār, tuṇiviyār cavuntiram ammi yār, celvi cintāmaṇi, aṭikaḷār eṉṉum nālvarik koṇṭa kuṭumpamāka iruntatu.
1911il pallava purattil kuṭipukum pōtu nīlāmpiki, tiruñāṉa campantam, māṇikkavācakam, tirunāvukkaracu, cuntara mūrtti eṉṉum makkaḷum koṇṭa oṉpatiṉmar kuṭumpamāka iruntatu. pallavuram pukunta piṉ tiripura cuntari piṟakkap patiṉmaṉ kuṭumpam āyiṟṟu. inniliyilēyē 27.08.1911il civat tuṟaviyarkkuriya tuṟavāṭi (kāvi) koṇṭu cuvāmi vētācalam āṉār. cuvāmi vētācalam eṉpataṉ taṉittamiḻākkamē maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ eṉpatu veḷippaṭi. muṉṉēyē avari aṭikaḷ eṉṟatu avartam māṇpu karutiyēyām.
mīḷpārvi
nām mīḷpārvi oṉṟupārkka vēṇṭum.
aṭikaḷ 21ām akaviyilēyē taṉittamiḻ āyvuttiṟam kivarap peṟṟār.
1903, 1907 ākiya āṇṭukaḷil mullippāṭṭu, paṭṭiṉappāli ākiya nūlkaḷukku āyvuri vaḻaṅkuṅkāl itiluḷḷa coṟkaḷ ittaṉi; ivaṟṟuḷ centamiḻccol ittaṉi; vēṟṟuc col ittaṉi - eṉa aṟutiyiṭukiṟār.
ātalāl, taṉittamiḻ eṉṉum tōṟṟam 1916 ām āṇṭil nīlāmpikiyār tūṇṭalāl ēṟpaṭṭami eṉpatu, akattē amintu kiṭanta vittu, muḷi viṭṭu veḷippaṭṭa niliyām. av veḷippaṭṭa niliyum uṭaṉē paṟṟip paṭaravilli! mēlum cila āṇṭukaḷiṉ piṉṉarēyē camacara caṉmārkka niliyam, potunilik kaḻakamākavum, ñāṉacākaram aṟivuk kaṭalākavum, cuvāmi vētācalam maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ ākavum paṭippaṭiyē kiḷarkiṉṟaṉavām!
ōr ariya amippu uruvākum niliyum, ōr ariya kaṇṭu piṭippu ulakavar aṟiya veḷippaṭalum, avi iyakkamākavum payaṉākavum ākiṉṟa niliyum paṟṟiya viḷakkam, aṭikaḷār taṉittamiḻ iyakka varalāṟṟil aṭaṅkiyuḷḷatām!
etiriṭit tūṇṭal: mēlum oṉṟu: oru nēriṭit tūṇṭaliṉum etiriṭit tūṇṭal valimi mikkatu eṉpatum, uḷḷattu uṟutiyi veḷḷap perukkāy veḷippaṭutta vallatu eṉpatum teḷivām.
oṉṟē āyiṉum taṉittamiḻ uṇṭā? eṉṟu kaṟṟaṟinta tamiḻar oruvarē viṉāvi nakittal, peṟṟa tāy moḻip piṟavikku uṟṟa takimi ākātu eṉpatum, ataṉi uṇarnta appiṟavi pāmpaṉ kurupāta aṭikaḷ pōl cēntaṉ centamiḻ eṉṉa oru nūliyaṟṟit tamiḻ taṉittu iyaṅkavalla taṉi uyar moḻi eṉṟu nilināṭṭiyiruppiṉ vaḻipaṭattakka piṟaviyāy amintirukkum! appēṟu ataṟku vāykkāmi,
tuṟantārkkut tuppuravu vēṇṭi maṟantārkol 
maṟṟi yavarkaḷ tavam	(kuṟaḷ 263)
eṉṉum vaḷḷuvar vāy moḻippaṭi,
taṉittamiḻt tantip pēṟu maṟimaliyaṭikaṭku vara iruppataṟkē nēriṭṭa nērcciyām eṉka.
iyakkam
aṭikaḷ iyakkam ālamarameṉak kālpala koṇṭu akala akala viriyat taṉittamiḻ iyakkamāy, ulakattamiḻk kaḻakamāy, pāvalarēṟu tamiḻk kaḷamāy, cērar koṟṟamāy, tamiḻkkāppuk kaḻakamāy, tamiḻp pātukāppuk kaḻakamāy, taṉittamiḻ ilakkiyak kaḻakamāy, tamiḻ vaḷarccik kaḻakamāy, maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ maṉṟamāy, tamiḻkkāval kuḻuvāy, iyaṟṟamiḻp payiṟṟakamāy, pāvāṇar tamiḻ iyakkamāy āṅkāṅku mukiḻttu muṉippoṭu kaṭaṉāṟṟa ēviyatām.
nūlakam
aṭikaḷ nūṟṟoki, arumaṇik kuviyaṉṉa nālāyiram nūlkaḷik koṇṭatu. etiratu uṇarntu, civacittānta nūṟpatippuk kaḻakattiṟku muṟkoṭiyāka vaḻaṅkiyatāl, nālāyiram nāṟpatāyiramāy mēlum mēlum virintu maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ nūlakap peyarāl, perumimiku vaḷḷalār mutaṟkaṇ poḻivu ceyta periyavar cōmu avarkaḷ illamē vāykka ulaka vaḷamākkiyatām! ataṉ vaḻiyākavē aṭikaḷ tāmē veḷiyiṭṭa nūlkaḷum, veḷiyiṭāmal kaḻaka vaḻi veḷivanta nūlkaḷum, aṭikaḷ kālattiṟkup piṉ veḷivanta nūlkaḷum ākiya muḻumit tokutikaḷum ki.pi. 1898 mutal 1969 vari mutaṟpatippāka veḷivantavi. avaṟṟuḷ paṭikaḷ kiṭṭutaṟku ariyaviyum uṇṭu. aṭikaḷ nūlkaḷ nāṭṭuṭimi ākkappaṭṭa pēṟṟāl, iṉṟu vāytta nūlkaḷ aṉittiyum oṭṭumottamāka vaḻaṅkum peruvāyppit tamiḻmaṇ patippakam peṟṟatām.
veḷḷac cētam
aṇmiyil ēṟpaṭṭa veḷḷap perukku patippakattuḷ pukuntu accu aṭikkappaṭṭatum, aṭikkappaṭa iruntatum, kaṇiṉippaṭutti vitta kuṟuvaṭṭukaḷum,  mūlanūlkaḷum eṉa vāri aḷḷik koṇṭu pōṉami, kaḷattukku vanta katir maṇikaḷ, kaḷañciyattiṟku vārāmal pōya, pēraḻivu oppatām. ātalāl, nūlkaḷi mīḷat tēṭavum, kaṇiṉippaṭuttavum, kuṟuvaṭṭu ākki acciṭavum eṉa mummaṭaṅkup paṇiyum pēriḻappum perutta avalamum uṇṭākkiṟṟām. anniliyil,
ūḻiyum uppakkam kāṇpar uliviṉṟit
tāḻā tuñaṟṟu pavar
eṉṉum vāymoḻiyi vāymi moḻiyākkiya tuṇivuc celvar iḷavaḻakaṉārum avar kuṭumpattavarum āvar!
muṉṉaṟivipput tiṭṭappaṭi nūlukku ārvamāyp paṇam celuttiyōrkkum nūl kālattil kiṭṭā uḷiccal illāmal pōkātē! avarkaḷum amintu tāṅkal, iyaṟki iyaṅkiyal vaḻiyatām.
veḷiyīṭukaḷ
tamiḻmaṇ patippakam tamiḻmoḻi iṉa maṇvaḷam - vaḷarcci mīṭṭeṭuppu - eṉpavaṟṟi muṉvittut tamiḻp pōrāḷi iḷavaḻakaṉārāl toṭaṅkappaṭṭatāl, pēriḻappukkām eṉat teḷivākat terintum, icik kalikku vāytta oppaṟṟa kaḷañciyamāy vāytta - karuṇāmirta cākaratti veḷiyiṭṭatām! muṉṉi vanta mutaṟpakuti pēriḻappākkiyum ataṉ piṉṉip pakutikaḷiyum aritiṉ muyaṉṟu veḷiyiṭṭatu! moḻiñāyiṟu pāvāṇar; īḻattu aṟiñar na.ci. kantiyā;  varalāṟṟaṟiñar cāmināta carmā, urivēntar auvi cu.tu; nāvalar na.mu.vē; paṇṭita vittuvāṉ ti.vē. kōpālar; cāmi citamparaṉār; mayili cīṉi. vēṅkaṭa cāmiyār; pēraṟiñar ka. veḷḷi vāraṇaṉār; pāvalar kuḻantiyār; pāvalar muṭiyaracaṉār; pāvēntar, tol poruḷ kalveṭṭu āyñar catācivaṉār, cāttaṉkuḷam irākavaṉ  nūlkaḷiyum eḷiyēṉ nūlkaḷiyum muḻumi muḻumiyāka veḷiyiṭṭatu.
tolkāppiyam- pāṭṭu - toki - kāppiyam - kīḻkkaṇakku - tamiḻnāṭṭu varalāṟu - āṅkilam valla aṟiñar irāma nātaṉār nūlkaḷi yum, yāḻppāṇa akarāti mutalām akarātit tokutikaḷiyum veḷi yiṭṭatu. patiṉeṭṭām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ piṟpakutitoṭṭu, irupattōrām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ muṟpakuti vari veḷivanta- miḻukku ākkamām nāṭṭu ṭimi ākkappaṭṭa nūlkaḷi ellām-ōriṭattup peṟṟu, oru taṉit tamiḻ nūlakam amikkum aḷavukku acciṭṭu vaḻaṅkiya perumi iḷavaḻakaṉārkku uṇṭu. avarē aṉṟi avar tuṇiyiyum makkaḷiyum uṟaviyum aṉpiyum naṇpiyum oruṅkē iṇittu, aṟiñar perumakkaḷ palar aravaṇippiyum vāyppak koṇṭu vāḻum iyalpu avarkku vāyttalāl maṭṭumē, ivvāṟu ceyaṟkariya ceyya vāyttatām eṉpataṟku eṭuttukkāṭṭākat tikaḻpavarum avar.
maṟimaliya māṇpu
maṟimaliyam, taṉittamiḻ iyakkam! tamiḻt taṉip peruvaḷam! muttamiḻc curappu! cīrtiruttac ceḻumi! vāḻviyal vaḷam! kalic collākkak kaḷañciyam! paṇṭit tamiḻar varalāṟṟup putiyal! āyviyal vaḻikāṭṭi! poḻivup polivu! eḻuttiṉ eḻil! moḻik kāval muṉiyam! muṭṭaṟukkum muḻutuṟu koṭi! mīṭṭeṭukkum mētakimi! aḷḷa aḷḷak kuṟiyā aṇimaṇik kuviyal! eṉal meymi viḷakkamām!
puttakam ēṟṟup polivatē puttakam
 vāḻiya nalaṉē! vāḻiya nilaṉē!
tiruvaḷḷuvar niliyam,	iṉpa aṉpuṭaṉ
7, irāmaṉ teru,	irā. iḷaṅkumaraṉ
tirunakar, maturi - 6
625006.

malikka vikkum maṟimaliyam!
patippuri
moḻiyē iṉamakkaḷ muṉṉēṟa nalla
vaḻiyām ataṉi vaḷarkka -paḻiyāyt
tamiḻayarat tāḻntāṉ tamiḻaṉ avaṉē
tamiḻuyarat tāṉuyarvāṉ tāṉ
moḻippēraṟiñar pāvāṇar avarkaḷ `moḻiyiṉ vaḻiyākavē moḻi pēcum makkaḷ muṉṉēṟa muṭiyum eṉak kūṟukiṟār. ulakam muḻutum iv vuṇmi uṇarappaṭṭuḷḷati varalāṟu kāṭṭukiṟatu.
pāvāṇari aṟivulakam cariyāka aṭiyāḷam kāṇpataṟkuk kāraṇamāṉatu maṟimaliyaṭikaḷār tanta pārāṭṭu!
maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ (15.7.1876 - 15.9.1950) tēvanēyap pāvāṇar (7.2.1902 - 16.1.1981) iruvarum taṉittamiḻiyakkattit tāṅki niṟkum tūṇkaḷākap pōṟṟappaṭupavarkaḷ.
maṟimaliyaṭikaḷār 1916il tōṟṟuvitta taṉittamiḻ iyakkam, nūṟṟāṇṭi eṭṭiyuḷḷa makiḻvāṉa nēram itu!
taṉittamiḻiyakka nūṟṟāṇṭi niṉivu kūrum vakiyil, taṉittamiḻiyakkat tanti maṟimaliyaṭikaḷāriṉ eḻuttu, coṟpoḻivu, nāṭ kuṟippu, kaṭitaṅkaḷ aṉittiyum `maṟimaliyam eṉum perun tokuppākat tamiḻmaṇ patippakam vaḻi veḷikkoṇarat tiṭṭamiṭṭōm.
ulakam muḻutum paravi vāḻum uṇarvu kuṉṟāta tamiḻarkaḷ tanta ūkkuvippu, intap peruntiṭṭatti niṟivēṟṟit tīravēṇṭum eṉum uṟutiyi eṉakkut tantatu.
taṉittamiḻ iyakka aṟiñarkaḷum ārvalarkaḷum aṭikaḷāriṉ ariya paṭippukaḷ palavaṟṟiyum tēṭittēṭi vaḻaṅki vaḻikāṭṭiṉar; valimiyūṭṭiṉar.
maṟimaliyaṭikaḷār 1898ām āṇṭiliruntu eḻuti vantuḷḷār. aṟivuk kaṭal (ñāṉacākaram) itaḻi 1902ām āṇṭiliruntu veḷiyiṭṭu vantuḷḷār. avariṉ paṭippukaḷ peruveḷḷamāy veḷivantirukkiṉṟaṉa.
accēṟātavi, peyar kūṭak kēṭṭaṟiyātavi, peyar terintum pār vikkuk kiṭṭātavi - eṉap palavakiyāyk kiṭittaṉa aṭikaḷāriṉ arum paṭippukaḷ! avaṟṟi `maṟimaliyam eṉum talippil 34 tokuppukaḷāka orucērap pārkkumpōtu, tamiḻulakam viyappaṭivatu uṟuti.
moḻinalam iṉanalam pēṇutal aṉṟō
muḻunalam peṟuvaḻi! nāmpeṟal eṉṟō?
ippaṭik kavaliyōṭu kēṭpār pāvalarēṟu peruñ cittiraṉār (10.3.1933 - 11.6.1995). avariṉ `teṉmoḻi itaḻ 1.8.1959 iliruntu veḷivarat toṭaṅkiya piṉputāṉ, taṉittamiḻt tici nōkki nūṟṟukkaṇakkāṉōr naṭipōṭat toṭaṅkiṉar. atil nāṉum oruvaṉ.
peruñcittiraṉāriṉ eḻuttu kāṭṭiya aṭiyāḷattāl, pāvāṇar nūlkaḷ eṉum aṟivupputiyal palar kikaḷilum kuṭiyēṟiyatu.
pāvāṇar nūlkaḷ valiyuṟuttip pēciyatāl, maṟimali yaṭikaḷārit tēṭippayilum ārvam tamiḻulakil puttuyir peṟṟatu.
āriyatti etirppatilum camaṟkirutatti vilakku vatilumē tamiḻiṉa mēmpāṭum tamiḻk kāppum aṭaṅkiyirukkiṟatu. avvuṇarcciyit tamiḻar neñcaṅkaḷil arumpa vittatu tirāviṭar iyakkam; malarntu viriyavittatu taṉittamiḻ iyakkam! kumuka, araciyal, paṇpāṭṭu malarccikku inta īriyakkaṅkaḷiṉ pēruḻippup payaṉpaṭṭatu.
tamiḻakattil taṉittamiḻ uṇarvu ki.mu. 7ām nūṟṟāṇṭukkup piṉ taḷarat toṭaṅkiyatu; 10ām nūṟṟāṇṭukkup piṉ cariyat toṭaṅkiyatu. vaṭamoḻi veḷḷattil tamiḻ iḻuttuc cellappaṭṭu viṭāmal kāppāṟṟiyōr kamparum pavaṇantiyum! piṉvantōrukku antak kāppuṇarcci illāmal, ayaṉ moḻip paṟṟukku aṭimiyāṉatāl tamiḻum tamiḻiṉamum peṟṟa iḻappukaḷ ērāḷam!
ayaṉmoḻic cēṟṟiliruntu tamiḻi mīṭkum muyaṟci āṅkilēyar varukiyiyoṭṭip puttuyir peṟṟatu. vaḷḷalār, pāmpaṉ cāmikaḷ, vaṇṇac carapam taṇṭapāṇi cuvāmikaḷ, cuppiramaṇiyacivā, paritimāṟ kaliñar mutaliya palarum toṭakka vitikaḷāyiṉar.
kōvi avināciyil piṟantu virutunakaril vāḻnta viruti civañāṉayōki 19.11.1908 ām nāḷ `tiruviṭar kaḻakam toṭaṅkit taṉittamiḻ uṇarccikku vēkaṅkūṭṭiṉār. avvamippil uṟuppiṉarāyiruntavar cuvāmi vētācalam (malimaliyaṭikaḷ).
maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ eṉat tam peyarik koṇṭu, 1916il avar toṭaṅki yatē taṉittamiḻ iyakkam.
`tuṟimiku āṅkilam maṟivaṭa moḻiyoṭu
 tūytamiḻp pēraṟiñar - taṉittamiḻ
 tōṟṟiya ōraṟiñar
pāvalarēṟu peruñcittiraṉār pāṭiyataṟkēṟpa, maṟimali yaṭikaḷār tamiḻ, āṅkilam, camaṟkirutam mutaliya paṉmoḻiyilum pulami mikkavar.
paṉmoḻip pēraṟiñarāṉa maṟimaliyaṭikaḷār tamiḻukkum tamiḻarukkum ātaravāka niṉṟatāl etirppukkum iḻappukkum āḷākkappaṭṭār. āṉālum uṟuti kuṉṟāmal tamiḻukku ātaravāy uḻikkum uḷḷattōṭiruntār. ataṉāl, tamiḻar uḷḷaṅkaḷil iṉṟaḷavum māṟāp perumiyōṭu avar vīṟṟirukkiṉṟār.
vāḻttāta nāḷilli viyakam
  maṟimali yaṭikaḷ maṟiyāt tiruppeyar
  vāḻttāta nāḷilli viyakam
pāvēntar pāratitācaṉ avar māṇpiṉip pāṭi makiḻntatum, tam `kuṭumpa viḷakku nūliṉi maṟimali yaṭikaḷukkuk kāṇikkiyākki vaḻaṅkiyatum, avariṉ uṟuti taḷarāta uḷḷamum uḻippum kaṇṭu pōṟṟiya naṉṟik kaṭaṉ eṉpatil ayyamilli.
ceṉṉiyil kaṭanta irupatu āṇṭukaḷākac ceyalpaṭṭu varum tamiḻmaṇ patippakap paṇikaḷit tamiḻulakam aṟiyum.
vāḻum talimuṟikkum varuṅkālat talimuṟikkum vaḻikāṭṭavalla tamiḻaṟiñarkaḷ palariṉ nūlkaḷi muḻumuḻut tokuppukaḷāka vaḻaṅkiyiruppatu tamiḻmaṇ patippakattiṉ muṭippiṉi (cātaṉi)yākap pōṟṟappaṭukiṟatu.
tamiḻ maṟumalarccikku vittiṭṭa aṟiñarkaḷik kaṇṭaṟintu, avarkaḷiṉ nūlkaḷi muḻumiyāṉa tokuppukaḷākat tamiḻmaṇ patippakam veḷiyiṭṭu varukiṟatu. avi kālam muḻutum payaṉpaṭavalla karuvi nūlkaḷ eṉpati aṟiñar ulakam aṟiyum!
tamatu nalatti otukki vittu, tamiḻ nalattik kākka vāḻnta aṟiñarkaḷiṉ aṉittu nūlkaḷiyum oru cērat tokuttu muḻut tokuppāka vaḻaṅkuvatu perumpaṇi! ataṉit tamiḻmaṇ patippakam toṭarntu niṟivēṟṟi varukiṟatu. tokuppu nūlkaḷiṉ paṭṭiyalip piṉṉiṇippil kāṇka.
tamiḻukkāka uḻikkum uṇarvi iḷamiyil emmiṭam ēṟpaṭuttiyōruḷ perumpulavar nakkīraṉārum avartam iḷaval pulavar cittiravēlaṉārum kuṟippiṭattakkōr! em naṉṟikkuriyōr!
taṉittamiḻ ārvam, tirāviṭar iyakka īṭupāṭu eṉum iru ciṟakukaḷāl emmi eḻavittavar perumpulavar nakkīraṉār! avari eṇṇik kaṇpaṉikka naṉṟi kūṟukiṟatu em neñcam.
kālappaḻamiyālum parāmarippuk kuṟivālum aḻiya nērnta tamiḻilakkiyaṅkaḷ aḷavaṟṟavi! avaṟṟil orupakutiyi yāvatu mīṭṭu nam kikaḷukku vaḻaṅkiyōr āṟumukanāvalar, ci.vi. tāmōtaraṉār, u.vē.cāminātar mutaliya cāṉṟōr perumakkaḷ!
tamiḻilakkiyap peruñcelvatti mīṇṭum mīṇṭum accākki, aṉivar kikaḷiyum eṭṭac ceyta perumi - civacittānta nūṟpatippuk kaḻakam, u.vē.cā. nūlakam, maṇivācakar patippakam mutaliya patippāḷarkaḷukku uriyatu.
accuttuṟi aṟimukamāṉa piṉpē, aṟivuttuṟi aṉivarukkum potuccottākum vāyppu vantatu. eṭṭāk kaṉiyāka ōlic cuvaṭikaḷil muṭaṅkik kiṭanta ilakkiyac celvaṅkaḷ, ellōrukkum eḷitāyk kiṭikkum paṇiyic ceyta patippāciriyarkaḷum, patippāḷarkaḷum nam naṉṟik kuriyōr!
iravu pakal pārātu mutumiyik karutātu uḻikkum maṉavuratti muṉṉōṭip patippāciriyarkaḷiṉ paṇiyē emakku vaḻaṅkiyatu.
aracum, palkalik kaḻakaṅkaḷum, aṟaniṟuvaṉaṅkaḷum, camaya pīṭaṅkaḷum ,perum iyakkaṅkaḷum, perum celvarkaḷum ceyya vēṇṭiya perun tamiḻppaṇi itu! eḷiyavarkaḷāl iyaṅkum tamiḻmaṇ patippakam peruntamiḻp paṇikaḷi iṉṉalkaḷukku iṭiyil arumuyaṟciyōṭu ceytu varuvatu, uḷḷavuram oṉṟiṉāltāṉ!
vallami cērkkum valimiyuṇ ṭākkum!
  vaṇṭamiḻ nintiṭil etunammik kākkum?
kākkum tamiḻik kākkum uṇarvip pāvēntar pāṭal varikaḷ tantaṉa.
accukku eṭṭāmalum kikkuk kiṭṭāmalum irunta pēraṟiñar palariṉ nūlkaḷi nāṭṭuṭimiyākki tirāviṭar iyakka aracukaḷ vaḻaṅkiṉa. ataṉāl, aṟivu paravalākum vāyppukaḷ uruvāyiṉa.
`carivum iḻappum namakku vantālum tamiḻukku varakkūṭātu eṉa eṇṇi uḻikkum iyakkaṅkaḷum amippukaḷum tamiḻ nāṭṭil vaṟṟāmal vaḷarntapaṭi irukkiṉṟaṉa.

tamiḻc cāṉṟōr pēravi (1998)
tamiḻmalarccikkum taṉittamiḻ vaḷarccikkum aṭittaḷamamitta ittaku amippukaḷukkumūlaviciyākattikaḻumperumiyip peṟuvōr maṟimali yaṭikaḷārum pāvāṇarumē!tamiḻvaḷattiṟkum tamiḻar nalattiṟkum civaṉiya mēmpāṭṭiṟkum maṟimaliyaṭikaḷār āṟṟiya paṇikaḷ aḷap pariyavi. avi toṭarpāka avar āyntaḷitta aṟivu muttukaḷ eṇṇaṟṟavi!
aṭikaḷāriṉ aṉittup paṭippukaḷum poruḷvaḻip pirikkappaṭṭu, kāla variciyil 34 tokuppukaḷākat tokukkap paṭṭu `maṟimaliyam eṉa uṅkaḷ kikaḷil ippōtu tavaḻkiṉṟatu.
pala talimuṟikaḷukkup payaṉpaṭum aṟivuk karuvūlam itu!
tamiḻar illantōṟum irukkavēṇṭiya aruntamiḻc celvam itu!
aṟivulakattāl `maṟimaliyam varavēṟkappaṭum eṉṉum nampikki emakkuṇṭu. nūṟ curaṅkattil nuḻiya aḻikkiṟōm.

patippāḷar vivaram
kō. iḷavaḻakaṉ 
piṟanta nāḷ		:	3.7.1948
piṟanta ūr		:	uṟantirāyaṉkuṭikkāṭu añcal					urattanāṭu vaṭṭam - 614 625							tañcāvūr māvaṭṭam.
kalvi		:	kallūri pukumuka vakuppu
ippōtiya toḻil		:	puḻakkattil illāta paḻantamiḻ nūlkaḷit 			tēṭiyeṭuttu veḷiyiṭal

āṟṟiyuḷḷa potuppaṇikaḷ
1965il paḷḷi māṇavaṉāka iruntapōtē moḻip pōrāṭṭattil muṉippākap paṅkēṟṟut taḷippaṭuttap peṟṟu 48 nāḷkaḷ ciṟiyil iruntavar.
piṟanta ūrākiya uṟantirāyaṉkuṭikkāṭṭil `ūrnalaṉ vaḷarccik kaḻakam eṉum camūka amippil iruntu ūr nalappaṇi āṟṟiyavar.
urattanāṭṭil `tamiḻar urimik kaḻakam eṉṉum amippiyum, pāvāṇar paṭippakattiyum naṇparkaḷuṭaṉ iṇintu niṟuvit tamiḻmoḻi, tamiḻiṉa, tamiḻaka mēmpāṭṭiṟku uḻittavar. iḷam talimuṟikkut tamiḻt toṇṭāṟṟiyavar.
pēraṟiñar aṇṇāviṉ matuvilakkuk koḷkiyi neñcil ēnti urattanāṭu matuvilakkuk kuḻuviṉ mukkiya amippāḷarkaḷil oruvarāka iruntu ceyalāṟṟiyavar. 1975-il tamiḻnāṭu caṭṭamaṉṟattil `urattanāṭu tiṭṭam eṉṟu pārāṭṭap peṟṟa matuvilakkut tiṭṭam veṟṟi peṟa uḻittavar.
tamiḻmaṇ patippakatti niṟuvi puḻakkattil illāta  paḻantamiḻ nūlkaḷiyum, putiya paṭippu ilakkiyaṅkaḷiyum, 19  ām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ iṟutiyilum 20ām nūṟṟāṇṭut toṭakkat tilum vāḻnta tamiḻccāṉṟōrkaḷ eḻuti vittuc ceṉṟa tamiḻiṉ aṟivuc celvaṅkaḷit tēṭi eṭuttu muḻumiyākac cērttu poruḷvaḻip pirittu, kāla niralil orē vīccil toṭar toṭarāka  veḷiyiṭṭut tamiḻnūl patippil taṉi muttiri patittu varupavar.
potunili
taṉittamiḻ iyakkat tanti maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ, tanti periyār, pāvēntar pāratitācaṉ, pēraṟiñar aṇṇā,  moḻinūl mūtaṟiñar ñā. tēvanēyap pāvāṇar,  pāvalarēṟu peruñcittiraṉār ākiyōri vaḻikāṭṭikaḷākak koṇṭu avartam koḷki kaḷi niṟivēṟṟa ayarātu uḻittu varupavar.

mutaṟkuṟaḷ vāta nirākaraṇam
mutaṟkuṟaḷakiya,
akara mutala eḻuttellām āti
  pakavaṉ mutaṟṟē ulaku
eṉpatu aṟiñarkaḷ pārviyil palatiṟap poruṭ kūṟukaḷukku iṭamāki iṉṟuvari toṭaravē ceykiṉṟatu.
parimēlaḻakar mutalām uriyāciriyarkaḷoṭu, putturi kāṇpāriyum orunilippaṭāvakiyil tarukkap poruḷākavē iruntu varukiṟatu.
āti pakavaṉ eṉpatu koṇṭu poruntā iḻimip puṉivum ulāvantatu! ammiyappaṉ eṉavum kāṇappaṭṭatu.
mutaṟkuṟaḷ uvami eṉappēraṟiñar ku. kōtaṇṭa pāṇiyārāl 200kku mēṟpaṭṭa pakka aḷavil āyvu nūlum veḷippaṭṭatu.
pakavaṉ eṉpatu `pakalavaṉ eṉpataṉ toki eṉavum palar karutiṉar.
tolkāppiyap pāyira uri pōl mutaṟkuṟaḷum aṟiñarkaḷiṉ tarukkap poruḷāy muṭinta muṭivu eṭṭāmal toṭaravē ceykiṉṟatu. mutaṟkuṟaḷ vāta nirākaraṇam eṉpatu aṭikaḷār kālam vari kāṇappaṭṭa urikaḷik kūṟi avaṟṟi maṟuttut tam kōḷ nāṭṭiyatākum. atu veḷippaṭṭa āṇṭu 1898.
- irā. iḷaṅkumaraṉ

tukaḷaṟu  pōtam
meykaṇṭār parampariyil vanta cīrkāḻic ciṟṟampala nāṭikaḷ eṉpavar iyaṟṟiya nūl itu. ivarik kāḻi paḻutikaṭṭi ciṟṟampala nāṭikaḷ eṉṟum kūṟuvar.
tukaḷ āvatu mācu malam kuṟṟam eṉappaṭuvatu. maṉamācukaḷil talippaṭṭatu. āṇavamalam. atu yāṉ eṉatu eṉṉum cerukku ākum. avvāṇavam akalutaṟkuriya aṟivuttiṟatti viḷaṅka urikkum meyyiyal nūlē tukaḷ aṟu pōtamām.
ciṟṟampala nāṭikaḷ pōtaka āciriyarākavum, pōtaka nūlāciriyarākavum tiruttoṇṭu ceyta perumiyar. civappayir vaḷartta cāṉṟōrkaḷuḷ oruvar. 14ām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉar; cīrkāḻi vāḻviṉar.
kāppuc ceyyuḷuṭaṉ 101 veṇpākkaḷi uṭiyatu innūl. mupporuḷām iṟi uyir taḷi innūl. mup poruḷām iṟi uyir taḷi eṉpavaṟṟi eḷitil viḷakkum ariya nūl.
ciṟṟampala nāṭikaḷiṉ māṇavarkaḷuḷ oruvar tattuvap pirakācar. avar iyaṟṟiya nūl tattuvap pirakācam, tukaḷaṟu pōtak kaṭṭaḷi eṉpavaṟṟiyum eḻutiṉār. civañāṉa pōtam mutalām nūlkaḷik kaṟṟut teḷiya vaḻikāṭṭum oḷiviḷakkaṉiya nūlkaḷ ivi eṉalām. maṟimaliyaṭikaḷār tukaḷaṟu pōtattiṟku urikaṇṭu 1898 il patippittār.
- irā. iḷaṅkumaraṉ




poruntum uṇavum poruntā uṇavum

1961il civacittānta nūṟpatippuk kaḻakam patippitta
nūliṉi mūlamākak koṇṭu innūl patippikkappaṭukiṉṟatu.
innūlip paṟṟiya kuṟippuri...
makkaḷiṉ nākarika vāḻviṟkum, uṭal tūymikkum, uḷattūymikkum, nōyaṟṟa peru vāḻvukkum, aṟivārnta iṉpattiṟkum innūl tuṇi niṟkiṟtu, poruntum uṇavu poruntā uṇavu, kollāmi, pulāl uṇṇāmi civa uṇavukku māṟāṉa koḷkikaḷiṉ maṟuppu eṉpaṉa kaṭṭuri amippukaḷākum.
iyaṟki vaḻaṅkum uṇavup poruḷkaḷiṉ uyvaṉavukaḷ kēṭuṟāta vakiyil avaṟṟip payaṉpaṭutta aṟivuṟuttukiṟār aṭikaḷār. pal vakip paṇṭaṅkaḷiyum māṟimāṟi uṭkoṇṭu palvaki uyvaṉavukaḷip peṟutalum, iṭam kālam uṭaliyalpu ākiyaṉa uṇarntu, cuvittuṇṭu vāḻntu uḻippatum nīṭiya nalvāḻvukku vaḻiyākum.
ūṉ uṉpatu uṭal tollikaḷip perukkum. payir paccikaḷiyum avaṟṟiṉ payaṉkaḷiyum uṇpatu uṭal nalaṉukku ukantatu. civa uṇavu koḷḷum iyalpiṉarē iṟi yuṇarviṉ iyaṟkikku icintu naṭappavarkaḷ; pulāl uṇpavarkaḷ payaṉpāṭṭaṟivum āṟṟalum peṟār eṉpaṉa aṭikaḷāriṉ uṇavuk koḷki kaḷām.
- ṭākṭar nā. ceyappirakācu
maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ ilakkiyappaṭippukaḷ (pak. 12)


Preface
 Of the three kingdoms in nature into which the created things are comprehensively classified, man finds his place fixed in the animal kingdom. Though the animal and the vegetable are alike in having, in common, life and growth, the one is not identical with the other but is distinguished from it by possessing the faculty of will potent to perform a complex series of actions for the sake of mintining its existence and preserving its life from the onslught of ferocious animals and other individuals of its own class. As for the vegetable naturally no such facilities seem to exist. Pressed by hunger when animals roam about in search of prey, or when a number of them, coming upon something not sufficient for them all to feed upon, engage themselves in a severe conflict over it, or when some of them, too weak and timid to withstand the attack of fierce kinds. flee away for life, one can see what a marvellous and tactful variety of actions denied to plant life they perform.
Since the vegetable kingdom is unlike the animal, in being left unprovided for its defence, it is clear that nature intended her to be used  for food by animals; while on the contrary she has forbidden one animal being preyed upon by another by implanting in each a passionate desire for preserving its life and by giving to each the means of accomplishing it. It may even be imagined that, in the remote past, nature could have made even the wild animals subsist solely upon herbs, fruits and roots and that it is only at a later period when certin large - sized and ferocious types of animals, belonging to polar regions where they had none of the vegetable products to live upon except animal flesh, migrated into genial southern climes, that the habit of preying upon the weaker ones became established in them. Once acquired this cruel habit came to be imitated by the animals of the tropical regions also , until it became a part and parcel of their nature.
But mother Nature never leaves unpunished those of her offspring that go astray from her control and learn to play foul towards her other children. As has been proved by the eminent Dr. A. Haig, all animal matter is saturated with uric acid poison, the fruitful source of numerous virulent types of diseases. Man or beast, all that take to animal diet import into their blood the poison of a very pernicious kind which not only undermines their own health but also tends to deteriorate both the mind and body of their progeny. In this manner all the flesh - eating creatures are made gradually to lose their vitality and become extinct at the end of a few centuries. To this hidden cuse that thus saps the essentials of life, must we attribute the extinction of the huge carnivorous animals  which in the past prehistoric ages were roving about the earth in large numbers. Even now how few in number are such harmful and worthsless animals as lions, tigers, bears, wolves and the like when compared with the swarms of such mild strong and useful animals as cattle, horse, sheep, deer and others that live entirely upon leaves and herbs.
Now, a problem of vital importance that concerns the lives of millions every year, remins unnoticed even by men of culture; for the source of terrible epidemics has not as yet been properly inquired into, and the remedial measures that are sought to bring relief to the suffering and the dying multitude do but touch the surface and not the depth of that source. As has been shown above, all animal carcasses being charged with extremely poisonous substance, it is from their exudations that infectious diseases of a terrible kind take their rise, spread rapidly everywhere and carry away millions of lives every year and every season. The disintegrated particles of putrid flesh continually escape into the air, find by this means an easy entrance into the living systems and produce pestilences varying in their action and character and frightful in their consequences. Whether of man or of beast every living flesh is apt to putrefy if not properly guarded aginst and a constant effusion of poisonous matter fills up the air until it spreads it to an extent of many hundred, nay even thousands of miles, in space. This festering property inherent in flesh requires to be held in check so long as the desire to perpetuate life continues. To do this effectually Nature has áovided us for food and medicine with different kinds of edible plants and fruits which alone are entirely free from uric acid poison. But unfortunately the major portion of mankind and animals of certin kinds have, as their staple food, adopted flesh obtined by the cruel and heartless act of killing the innocent and most useful animals all over the world. Every cruel deed brings with it its own punishment. As pointed out above. Nature has put into the core of all animal matter a dreadfull poison - dreadful alike to those who look into its silent workings and to those who overlook them in their rapacious craving for meat diet which is working out the ruin of all who thwart her benign purposes and violate her natural laws. With the dily consumption of meat - diet, man constantly receives into his blood an infusion of animal poison and thereby arguments the festering process that is already going on within his own flesh.
What a foolish creature man is with all his learning and ingenious inventions to add poison to poison and kill others as well as himself! Instead of taking the wholesome dish of vegetable meal that produces pure, rich and vital blood and preserves flesh from putrefaction, he continually gorges stinking meat, until he falls ill when as a matter of necessity he abstins from the unnatural diet and seeks as a curative means medicines prepared from plants and herbs. Even then the abnormal craving for meat is so strong in many that they entreat their physician to allow them the use of light and easily digestible animal food and prescribe at the same time some powerful portion to assist the digestion of such strong food. And many a physician being more particular about his fees than the health of his patients, dares not lose his professional call but acceds to their entreaties and goes on prescribing medicines one after another along with chicken broth and meat-soup which, contining as they do, a great quantity or urinc acid poison, only tend to increase the gravity of the patients’ sick condition.
In other cases where the patient is a strict vegetarian and the attendant doctor a flesh-eater, the doctor invariably induces the patient to use beef-soup and port wine even aginst his wish. If, by chance or through some mysterious cuse which still reamins unknown to the scientific world, the patient recovers his health, the doctor is congratulated - why he congratulates himself, on the success of his treatment and from that time forward he not only gins the confidence of other suffering people but even believes in his own professional skill to a degree that he makes it a point to extend without any hesitation the benefit of his clever prescription of meat-soup to his other patients also. But if, contrary to his expectations, the patient expires, which occurs in six or seven cases out of ten, he attributes it to the patient’s low vitality or to some trivial mistakes committed either by the helpless man or by his relatives, he himself not knowing that his patient’s death was brought about by the very beef-soup and egg-flip that he prescribed. And strangely enough in the course of a few days following the death of his victim, the doctor takes care to collect his fees from the relatives of the dead man. Such alas! is the doctor’s profession and such the fate of those whoseek his help! And yet, what is the real cuse of his death? May it not be the blood-poison augmented to an extent so as to affect the bodily functions by a frequent introduction of more poison in the shape of meat soup and wine into the life-fluid that had already been rendered impure by the disease? Even the lower carnivorous animals, when they are taken ill, abstin from flesh and fast for days together taking at intervals only herbs and roots until they are completely recovered. A careful observer of animal life can never fail to notice this instinctive act on theri part to rid themselves of the accumulation of blood impurities. Even among us the innocent and hard working people who live in villages removed far away from the contamination of town-life, very seldom feel unwell and when they do so they invariably fast until their appetite is improved. In a day or two they get better simply becuse their chief food consists of light gruel and cheap and wholesome vegetables, animal food being very scarce in rural parts. In this manner their - recovery from any occasional illness is speedy and sure and they live to a good old age.
This simple, harmless, and healthy life led instinctively by country folks and lower animals during the period of illness must have formed a model lesson to the town people who boast of learning and enlightenment, rank and civilization. What avileth all learning, light and rank, if one’s life is rendered unfit for the enjoyment of physical, moral, intellectual and spiritual pleasures and be cut off prematurely by sickness and sorrow! It is, therefore, absolutely necessary that every one should know the proper means of right living and adopt it at any cost. All the physical troubles now existing might be traced to the root cuse of blood -poison called otherwise Uric Acid. It is this poison that produces multifarious kinds of diseases by being carried into the vital fluid of the human frame through flesh foods. To avoid blood poison, one must totally avoid meat food which is penetrated with uric acid poison.
Observe too how good, how mild, how useful are those animals that feed upon grass and straw, leaves and roots and how bad, how fierce and how worthless those that live upon flesh food. Do not bulls and cows, horses and camels, sheep and deer live only upon grass, and straw, leaves and reeds rejected by man as useless? And yet, how strong, how clean, and how serviceable they are to man who treats them mercilessly! Can any one make lions, tigers, wolves and panthers as useful, as these? It is impossible and impracticable. Man who has climbed up the ladder of evolutionary process so as to become possessed with a tender heart and a thinking mind which form the foundation of his moral character, should have bestowed his thgought on the intimate relation in which he stands to lower animals which by serving him in many ways, make his existence happy and comfortable and have, therefore, a strong clim on him to be treated kindly and protected from being slughtered. Dimmed by impetuous passions and strong appetite the mind of carnivorous animals can pay no heed to the value and existence of other lives as they do to their own, but man who has risen far above them in point of culture and refinement of feelings should not have been so carried away by such brutal passions and appetency as to lose sight of the great value which every moving being sets so ardently on its own life and existence.
In what does the excellence of man consist? Is it not in the freedom of his will, in the power he can freely exercise to discriminate between what is good and what is evil? Is it not in the tenacity of his resolve to cling to that alone which brings happiness not only to him but to all moving beings that have a right to live being possessed like him with an instinct of self-preservation?  This right given to every creature not by man but by Providence, this right to live in an organism even the seemingly trivial but truly marvellous stgructure of which the combined intelligence of all the scientific men in the world cannot rise, this right which each and evry moving creature possesses for the purpose of promoting  the growth of its understanding and enlivening its capacity for enjoyment, if man, in his eagerness to fill his stomach, would still refuse to recognize, then the supreme ethical importance that is attached to his existence in the universal conception of the moral law becomes empty and meaningless; for, by this disregard he descends lower down in the scale of culture and delicacy of moral sense and joins the brute creations. It is, therefore, absolutely necessary that, in order to mintin his ethical status and widen the scope it affords him for a freer and fuller play of all his finer sensibilities which not only secure him aginst the troubles incident to lower forms of lives but also increase the extent and intensity of his happiness, he must totally avoid infringing on the rights of other moving lives and adhere strictly to pure vegetable diet alone.
But it is to be borne in mind that even among the articles of vegetarian diet there are those that contin proportionately a greater quantity of uric acid poison then is found in the meat preparations. Such are tea, coffee, cocoa etc. These and stimulants like alcohol, tobacco, opium and others must be studiously avoided by all who wish to lead a healthy and long life and to produce and perpetuate a long line of descendants strong and sound in both mind and body. Of the substances that compose a wholesome vegetarian meal entirely free from uric acid poison, the following six classes of foodstuffs are mentioned by Dr. A. Haig as sufficient to build up all the constituents of a sound and vigorous human body. They are 1. Milk and its products 2. Bread, cereal foods and gluten 3. Nuts and nut-foods 4. Garden vegetables as potatoes 5. Garden fruits as apples 6. Dried and foreign fruits. In addition to being cheap, these are sweet to taste, sweet to semll, pleasing to mind, harmless and humane and using them as food is in complete harmony with the high ethical station of life to which man has arisen after so many hard and fierce life and death struggles. May it therefore behave every man and every woman to awaken to a sense of the value of their ethical life and lead it so as not to cuse any harm either to man or any innocent animal! And be it remembered that there can be no harm greater than that of killing a living being and depriving it of the life given not by man but by providence for the great benefit of improving its understanding by a knowlede and experience of the world!
The above was contributed by me as an article to The Indian Humanitarian magazine conducted by The Bombay Humanitarian League and appeared in the 5th no. of its first volume, in January, 1920. For the benefit of those of our countrymen who wish to read everything in English instead of in their own mothertongue, it is re-published here with many alterations and a few additions. For this I owe my obligation to the above-mentioned League which by publishing the HUmanitarian magazine and numerous books, booklets and pamphlets, has advanced the cuse of vegetarianism and brought immense relief to our much useful domestic animals by stopping many animal-sacrifices and converting thousands of our people to vegetarianism. Those who wish to go deeper into this vitally important subject of vegetarianism will do well to go through all the publications of this League and make themselves thoroughly acquainted with the inestimable knowledge they yield of right living.
Now, the Tamil work that appears in the following pages consists of four parts of which the first two are taken from my large Tamil work on ‘Long Life’. The third part which deals with the huimanitarian teachings of the great religions of the world was written many years ago and it appeared in many periodicals that were then in existence. It is now inserted here, so that it may be of permanent use to the Tamil reading public. The fourth and the last part is newly written to meet in a plin scientific spirit the arguments rised aginst vegetarianism by the adherents of the Tamil atheistic movement that is now set of foot.
Pallavaram									         Vedachalam
 										               9th May 1929.
